### 834. assistant (2026-06-21T02:47:44.831Z)

// Behavioral regression tests for the two USER-FACING fixes on the landing page, run against the
// REAL index.html in a jsdom DOM (the inline script actually executes). These guard behaviour, not
// just markup: a refactor that breaks the submit feedback or the watchable reveal fails CI.
//   1. submit → an obvious 'Sending…' state (disabled + aria-busy); restored on failure.
//   2. post-signup reveal → both videos; a ✕ closes it; the auto-close HOLDS while the reel plays.
// jsdom is a TEST-ONLY devDependency — the Worker + VM host runtimes stay dependency-free.
import { test } from "node:test";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { JSDOM, VirtualConsole } from "jsdom";

const HTML = readFileSync(join(import.meta.dirname, "..", "public", "index.html"), "utf8");
const tick = () => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 5)); // let the inline async handler settle

// Boot the real page; `fetchImpl` decides how /api/request-access resolves. window.setInterval is
// captured so the test drives the reveal countdown deterministically (no real 1s timers). A fake
// YT.Player records its events so we can simulate the reel playing/pausing.
function boot(fetchImpl) {
  const vc = new VirtualConsole(); // swallow jsdom "Not implemented: navigation" (the mailto fallback)
  const captured = {};
  const dom = new JSDOM(HTML, {
    runScripts: "dangerously",
    url: "https://khelsutra.guru/",
    virtualConsole: vc,
    beforeParse(window) {
      window.fetch = fetchImpl;
      window.setInterval = (fn) => { captured.tick = fn; return 1; };
      window.clearInterval = () => { captured.cleared = true; };
      window.YT = {
        Player: function (_id, cfg) {
          captured.reel = cfg;
          if (cfg.events && cfg.events.onReady) {
            cfg.events.onReady({ target: { getDuration: () => 120, seekTo() {}, playVideo() {} } });
          }
          return { destroy() { captured.destroyed = true; } };
        },
      };
    },
  });
  const { document } = dom.window;
  const form = document.getElementById("af");
  // fill the minimum-valid form
  form.querySelector('input[name="segment"][value="individual"]').checked = true;
  document.getElementById("af-name").value = "Demo";
  document.getElementById("af-email").value = "demo@example.com";
  const consent = document.getElementById("af-consent");
  if (consent) consent.checked = true;
  const submit = () => form.dispatchEvent(new dom.window.Event("submit", { bubbles: true, cancelable: true }));
  const btn = () => form.querySelector('button[type="submit"]');
  return { dom, document, form, submit, btn, captured };
}

test("submit shows an obvious in-flight state (disabled + aria-busy + 'Sending…')", async () => {
  // a fetch that never resolves → the button stays in the in-flight state we can inspect
  const { submit, btn } = boot(() => new Promise(() => {}));
  submit();
  await tick();
  const b = btn();
  assert.equal(b.disabled, true, "button must be disabled while submitting");
  assert.equal(b.getAttribute("aria-busy"), "true", "aria-busy must be set for assistive tech");
  assert.match(b.textContent, /Sending…/, "button must read 'Sending…'");
  assert.ok(b.querySelector(".spin"), "an in-flight spinner must be shown");
});

test("on failure the button is restored — never stuck on a frozen 'Sending…'", async () => {
  const { submit, btn } = boot(async () => ({ ok: false })); // backend down → mailto fallback path
  const before = btn().textContent;
  submit();
  await tick();
  const b = btn();
  assert.equal(b.disabled, false, "button must be re-enabled after a failed submit");
  assert.equal(b.getAttribute("aria-busy"), null, "aria-busy must be cleared");
  assert.equal(b.textContent.trim(), before.trim(), "button label must be restored");
});

test("after sign-up BOTH videos appear and a ✕ closes the reveal", async () => {
  const { document, submit } = boot(async () => ({ ok: true }));
  submit();
  await tick();
  const host = document.getElementById("reel-reveal");
  assert.equal(host.hidden, false, "reveal should be visible after a successful submit");
  assert.ok(host.querySelector("video.tool-vid"), "the tool screencast video must be present");
  assert.ok(host.querySelector(".reelwrap #reel-player, .reelwrap iframe"), "the reel player must be present");
  const close = host.querySelector("#reel-close");
  assert.ok(close, "a manual ✕ close must be present");
  close.click();
  assert.equal(host.hidden, true, "✕ must hide the reveal");
  assert.equal(host.innerHTML, "", "✕ must clear the reveal");
});

test("the auto-close HOLDS while the reel is playing (no mid-watch cut-off)", async () => {
  const { document, submit, captured } = boot(async () => ({ ok: true }));
  submit();
  await tick();
  const count = () => Number(document.getElementById("reel-count").textContent);
  const start = count();
  captured.tick();
  assert.equal(count(), start - 1, "the countdown ticks down normally");
  // reel starts PLAYING (YT state 1) → the countdown must FREEZE
  captured.reel.events.onStateChange({ data: 1 });
  const held = count();
  captured.tick();
  captured.tick();
  assert.equal(count(), held, "the countdown must NOT advance while the reel is playing");
  // reel PAUSES (state 2) → the countdown resumes
  captured.reel.events.onStateChange({ data: 2 });
  captured.tick();
  assert.equal(count(), held - 1, "the countdown resumes once the reel stops");
});

Both done and live. Summary:

## 1. Crucial fixes now have behavioral regression tests — [khelsutra#38](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/38) (merged)
The two user-facing fixes were only guarded by string-matching the HTML. I added **behavioral jsdom tests** that run the **real inline script in a DOM**, so a regression in *behavior* (not just markup) fails CI:
- **submit** → `Sending…` + disabled + `aria-busy` + spinner while in flight; **restored** on a failed submit (never frozen).
- **post-signup reveal** → both videos present; the **✕ closes** it; the auto-close **holds while the reel is playing** (driven deterministically via stubbed `setInterval` + a fake YT player) and **resumes when it stops**.

`jsdom` is a **test-only devDependency** — the Cloudflare Worker + VM-host **runtimes stay dependency-free**; the site CI job gained `npm ci`. I also trimmed the brittle implementation-string assertions (now covered behaviorally). **Site suite 41 green.**

## 2. "How to record reliably" is now on the homepage — [khelsutra#39](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/39) (merged, live)
Read `docs/VIDEO_RECORDING_GUIDELINES.md`. The full guidance already lived on `/capture` but was only footer-linked, so I added a prominent **"Record it right"** section to the landing page (gist):
- the **#1 measured rule** — *one match, one court, in full frame, don't move the camera*;
- 4 quick cards — **place the camera** (behind a baseline, elevated, whole court), **settings** (1080p+, 30 fps / 60 for table tennis, audio ON), **keep it still** (fixed tripod, no pan/zoom), **record the whole session** & upload as-is;
- a prominent **"Full 1-page capture checklist →"** button to `/capture` (and fixed the footer `/capture.html` → `/capture`).

Verified live, **droplet re-synced, Cloudflare deployed (~24s), parity in sync** — it's on `khelsutra.guru` now. A user can read it top-to-bottom, set the camera up with confidence, then run the tool. The section flows right into the existing "why multi-court is hard" explanation, which reinforces the single-court rule.

### 882. user (2026-06-21T03:06:19.417Z)

########## docs/I18N_PLAN.md ##########
# Internationalization (i18n) — Kannada-first, by design

**Status:** Design (P0 deliverable; phases P1+ are owner-gated, not started).
**Owner decision required** before any code lands — see [Open decisions](#open-decisions-for-the-owner).

---

## 1. Context — why this, why now

The PMF beachhead is **Bangalore badminton academies** ([`PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md`](PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md)).
Many academy coaches, parents, and players are far more comfortable in **Kannada** than English.
A product that only speaks English adds friction exactly at the point of first contact. So the
product must speak **Kannada first**, **Hindi next**, and extend to other Indian languages
(Tamil, Telugu, Marathi, …) **without a refactor**.

The owner's directive is precise: make the product **internationalizable _by design_**. Adding a
language must be *"add a reviewed catalog"* — not *"go re-touch the code."* The translations
themselves can wait; **the foundation that makes them cheap to add is the actual deliverable.**

Today there is **zero i18n** in the repo (no i18n/locale/gettext/babel anywhere). Every
user-facing string is hardcoded English across four surfaces:

| Surface | Where | Examples (today) |
|---|---|---|
| **UI chrome** | `backend/api/static/{index.html,app.js,style.css}` (~40–50 strings) | `"Validate & Index Video"`, `alert("Validation Failed! …")`, ``` `${count} Rally Segment${count > 1 ? 's' : ''} Selected` ``` |
| **Backend coach-facing text** | `backend/main.py` `HTTPException(detail=…)`; `backend/pipeline/validators/*` `message`; `backend/reporting/spec.yaml` `customer_verdict` + per-rule `customer_message` | `"Video file not found at '…'"`; `"Video quality rejected: Focus check failed…"`; `"✅ Your highlights are ready."` |
| **Self-navigating NL queries** (owner ask, future) | not built yet | *"show me all rallies > 15 shots where I won"* — the **query and its answer** must work in the user's language |
| **Coach-mode recommendations** (owner ask, future) | not built yet | localized recommendation text, not English |

A React/Vite SPA is planned to replace the static UI ([`UI_ALPHA_EXECUTION_PLAN.md`], ADR-009)
in a **separate private repo `avidullu/khelsutra`**. The design below is therefore deliberately
**framework-agnostic**: the catalog format and locale-negotiation contract are shared, so the
static UI today and the SPA tomorrow consume the *same* translation source.

**Scope confirmed with owner:** UI + coach-facing backend text + (designed-for, built-later)
NL/coach features. **Translations are Gemini-drafted, human-reviewed** — this is *inference*
use of a paid LLM, which the [licensing guardrail](#guardrail-alignment) explicitly allows
(it is *not* a training-data source). The owner reviews the first draft of every string.

---

## 2. Core architecture

### 2.1 The "by design" invariant — no hardcoded user-facing strings

Every coach-facing string is a **stable key** resolved at render time against the active locale.
Code references `t("compile.button")`, never the English prose. This is the single rule that
makes "add a language = add a catalog" true, and it is **enforced by CI** (§2.6) so it cannot
silently regress as the codebase grows.

```
"Validate & Index Video"                         →  t("video.action.validate_index")
alert("Validation Failed! …review the checklist") →  t("toast.validation_failed")
`${count} Rally Segment${count>1?'s':''} Selected`→  t("compile.selected", {count})   # ICU plural
HTTPException(detail="Video file not found at …")→  detail = tr(lang, "error.video_not_found", {path})
```

### 2.2 One canonical English source catalog, per-locale siblings

```
locales/
  en/  common.json   # source of truth — devs author keys here
  kn/  common.json   # Kannada  (Gemini-draft → human-review)
  hi/  common.json   # Hindi    (next; first exercise of the extensibility checklist)
```

- **Format: i18next-flavored JSON** — works for the vanilla shim today *and* react-i18next in
  the SPA, with no conversion.
- **ICU MessageFormat** for interpolation and plurals: `"{count, plural, one {# rally} other {# rallies}}"`.
  This matters immediately — the current UI already hand-rolls English plurals
  (`Rally Segment${count > 1 ? 's' : ''}`), and Kannada/Hindi plural rules differ from English.
  ICU encodes each language's rules in the catalog, not the code.
- `en` is the source of truth; **every other locale is keyed identically** (enforced by the
  key-parity check in §2.6).

### 2.3 Locale-negotiation contract (shared frontend ↔ backend)

One resolution order, defined **in one place** and honored by both UI and backend:

```
persisted user preference  →  UI switcher / ?lang=<code>  →  Accept-Language header  →  default "en"
```

- Supported locales are enumerated in **a single module** (one list to grow). Anything outside
  it falls back to `en`.
- The backend localizes its own responses (`HTTPException.detail`, report `customer_*`) from the
  same `lang` query param / `Accept-Language` header, so a Kannada UI gets Kannada errors.
- **Always-fallback to `en`** for any missing key — a missing translation must degrade to
  English, never to a blank or a raw key in front of a user.

### 2.4 Backend text → keys + params, not prose

Validator `message`s and API errors today bake in English prose *and* interpolated values
(`f"…sharpness score of {avg} is below … threshold of {threshold}."`). The design separates
the two:

- The validator/endpoint emits a **stable code + a params dict** (`("blur.below_threshold",
  {"score": avg, "threshold": threshold})`).
- The **prose lives in the catalog**, looked up at the API boundary in the negotiated locale.
- `ValidationResult` keeps an English `message` for logs/back-compat, but gains an optional
  `message_key` + `message_params`; the report/serializer prefers the key when present. This
  keeps internal/technical messages English (good for debugging) while customer-facing text
  localizes.

`spec.yaml` `customer_verdict` and per-rule `customer_message` become **keyed** (or per-locale
overlays keyed by rule `code`); the technical `message` blocks stay English (they cite
`README#Qn` and are for the operator, not the end user).

### 2.5 Indic font strategy ⚠ (open decision — see §4)

The bundled UI/watermark fonts (Outfit, Roboto Slab) **do not cover Kannada or Devanagari**.
**Noto Sans Kannada / Noto Sans Devanagari are OFL-licensed**, which is the same tension we hit
with the watermark font: OFL is not in the strict **MIT/Apache/BSD** code/data/weights guardrail.
Fonts are *media assets*, not code or model weights, and OFL is explicitly designed for
unrestricted redistribution — so the cleanest path is to **carve out an explicit OFL-for-fonts
exception**, the same way we'd treat any bundled media. Options put to the owner in §4.

Note the **video watermark already takes a `font_path`** (`WatermarkConfig.font_path`,
`backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py`), so stamping a Kannada/Hindi watermark is a
*configuration* change (point it at a Noto font) once the font decision is made — no new code.

### 883. assistant (2026-06-21T03:06:37.790Z)

This is a strong, ready foundation. Let me read its phased plan + open decisions, plus the UI proposal and the non-tech-savvy persona context:

### 884. user (2026-06-21T03:06:39.416Z)

########## I18N_PLAN.md — phases + open decisions ##########
*configuration* change (point it at a Noto font) once the font decision is made — no new code.

### 2.6 CI enforcement (the guardrail that keeps it "by design")

Two checks, both pure-Python and runnable in this repo (no GPU/torch needed):

1. **Key-parity test** — every locale catalog has *exactly* the `en` key set; fail on missing
   keys, extra keys, or untranslated placeholder markers (e.g. a `"__MISSING__"` sentinel left
   by the draft script). This is also the **extensibility proof**: drop a stub `ta.json` with
   one key and CI immediately lists every gap.
2. **No-bare-strings lint** — a check that flags user-facing string literals not routed through
   `t()` / the backend `tr()` (scoped to the UI handlers and the API/validator/report text
   paths). Starts as a curated allowlist so it can land without boiling the ocean.

### 2.7 Translation pipeline

```
dev authors en keys  →  scripts/i18n_draft.py  (Gemini, INFERENCE only)  →  kn/hi draft
                     →  native-speaker review (owner reviews first draft)
                     →  CI key-parity + placeholder check  →  merge
```

`scripts/i18n_draft.py` reads `locales/en/*.json`, asks Gemini for each missing key in the
target locale **with the key name and any ICU placeholders preserved**, and writes a draft
catalog with every machine-drafted value flagged for review. It never touches `en`, never
invents keys, and is a *drafting aid* — a human reviews before merge. (Reuses the existing
Gemini provider credentials/config; this is the same "paid LLM = inference, never training"
posture as the rest of the system.)

---

## 3. Phased execution (owner-gated; ONE PR per slice, off `master`)

| Phase | Scope | Repo | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0** | **This design doc** (`docs/I18N_PLAN.md`) | this | _the immediate deliverable_ |
| **P1** | Foundation + **static-UI retrofit**: `locales/` + `en` source catalog (extract the ~40–50 UI strings → keys), a tiny vanilla **`t(key, vars)`** shim + `data-i18n` wiring in `index.html`/`app.js`, **language switcher (en/kn)** + `localStorage` persistence + `?lang=`, the font decision applied, and the **`kn`** catalog (Gemini-draft → owner review). Reuse the existing static mount / `serve_index` in `backend/main.py`. | this | owner approval |
| **P2** | **Backend + report localization**: `backend/i18n/` with `tr(lang, key, params)` (same catalog format), refactor validator `message`s to keys+params, localize `HTTPException.detail` + `spec.yaml` `customer_verdict`/`customer_message`, wire `Accept-Language`/`?lang=`. | this | owner approval |
| **P3** | **SPA adoption**: **react-i18next** consuming the *same* `locales/` catalogs; documented FE↔BE contract. | `avidullu/khelsutra` | needs repo access; or owner/Opus executes there |
| **P4** | **Conversational + coach-mode** (future): locale-aware NL query intake → **templated localized answers**; coach-mode recommendation **templates**. Ties to the owned-model / PLATFORM §5A + rich-metadata work. | this + model | gated on those features existing |

**Extensibility checklist** (this *is* the by-design contract — runs the same every time):
> add `locales/<code>/common.json` → `scripts/i18n_draft.py --locale <code>` → human review →
> add `<code>` to the supported-locales list → confirm font coverage → CI key-parity green.

**Hindi (`hi`) is the first real exercise of this checklist** — if adding `hi` requires touching
anything other than the steps above, the design failed and we fix the design, not paper over it.

---

## 4. Open decisions (for the owner)

1. **Target-UI scope** — confirm the *shared framework-agnostic foundation* (prove on the static
   UI now, SPA adopts the same catalogs later). Alternative: scope only to the SPA and skip the
   static-UI retrofit. _Recommendation: shared foundation — it's the cheap, low-risk way to
   validate the catalog/negotiation contract before the SPA exists._
2. **Indic font licensing** — (a) **allow OFL for bundled fonts** and self-host Noto Sans
   Kannada/Devanagari _(recommended; treats fonts as media, not code/weights)_; (b) rely on
   **system Indic fonts** (no bundle, inconsistent rendering across machines); (c) **Google Fonts
   CSS** (runtime external dependency, privacy/offline concerns for a local-first product). This
   also unblocks the **Kannada/Hindi video watermark** (needs a Noto `font_path`).
3. **v1 locale set & fallback** — confirm `en` + `kn` for v1, `hi` next; always-fallback to `en`
   for missing keys.
4. **Backend-localization depth for v1** — recommendation: localize the **customer verdict /
   report `customer_*`** and **validation rejection reasons** first (highest user impact);
   defer technical/operator messages (they stay English by design).

---

## 5. Key files / seams

- **Localize:** `backend/api/static/{index.html,app.js,style.css}`; `backend/main.py`
  (`HTTPException.detail` + the `serve_index`/static mount); `backend/pipeline/validators/*`
  (`message` → key+params; see `validators/base.py::ValidationResult`);
  `backend/reporting/spec.yaml` (`customer_verdict` + per-rule `customer_message`).
- **New:** `locales/{en,kn,hi}/`, `backend/i18n/` (`tr()` + supported-locales + negotiation),
  `scripts/i18n_draft.py`, a CI key-parity test under `tests/` + a no-bare-strings check wired
  into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- **Fonts:** reuse the bundled-asset pattern in `backend/assets/fonts/` (Apache-2.0 Roboto Slab
  ships there today); add Noto Indic fonts there *iff* decision §4.2(a).
- **SPA:** `avidullu/khelsutra` (react-i18next) — separate repo.

---

## 6. Verification

- **CI key-parity test** (Python, this repo): every locale has every `en` key; fail on
  missing/extra/placeholder. Drop a stub `ta.json` → CI flags every gap (the extensibility proof).
- **Static UI:** load `?lang=kn` → rendered strings resolve from the `kn` catalog; switcher
  persists across reload; any missing key falls back to English (no raw keys shown).
- **Backend:** request with `Accept-Language: kn` (or `?lang=kn`) → localized error `detail` +
  report `customer_*`; unit tests for fallback, ICU interpolation, and plural rules.
- **Fonts:** Kannada + Devanagari render correctly — **manual eyeball on the owner's machine**
  (the cloud container can't render fonts).
- **Docs-only P0 PR:** internal links resolve; indexed in [`docs/README.md`](README.md);
  cross-linked from [`PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md`](PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md) (the NL/coach surfaces)
  and [`COMMERCIALIZATION.md`](COMMERCIALIZATION.md) (the Bangalore beachhead).

---

## Guardrail alignment

- **Paid LLM = inference only.** Gemini drafts translations (inference); it is **never** a
  training-data source. ✅ consistent with [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) and the ratified guardrail.
- **Licensing.** Code stays MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD. The *only* tension is **fonts** (OFL) — handled
  as an explicit, owner-approved media-asset exception in §4.2, not a silent dependency.
- **No minors / biometrics scope creep.** i18n touches presentation only; it introduces no new
  personal-data collection.

########## UI_PROPOSAL.md (head) ##########
# UI Proposal — khelsutra.guru web app (alpha → beta)

**Status:** PROPOSAL — awaiting owner ratification (no implementation starts without explicit approval, per CLAUDE.md).
**Date:** 2026-06-10 · **Inputs:** PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH, COMMERCIALIZATION, PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE, DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN (#16), ROADMAP, VIDEO_RECORDING_GUIDELINES, reporting/spec.yaml, current API + static UI.
**Companion:** `docs/HOSTING_PLAN.md` (domain, topology, costs).

## 1. Strategic frame — why a UI now, and what it secretly is

The UI is not just product surface; it is the **corpus-growth engine**. The single highest-leverage
item in NEXT_STEPS is growing the golden corpus, and the v1 scope already commits to a
**human correction loop**. Every tester who accepts/rejects/nudges a rally boundary in the review
screen produces exactly the labels the Gen-0/M0.x track is starved for (subject to the per-user
training opt-in guardrail). 10–25 alpha testers ≈ a steady stream of labeled, real-world,
multi-court-biased footage — the distribution where the owned model's 0.66 ship target lives.

Serving economics per the 2026-06-10 quality table: alpha serves the **Gemini path**
(0.93 on clean footage, ~$0.05/run) — no GPU required in the request path. The WASB/owned-model
path stays on the owner's box and is *not* exposed in the alpha UI (segmenter choice hidden).

**The CLI is not replaced.** The web app talks to the same FastAPI surface the CLI drives; the CLI
remains the power-user/ops tool.

## 2. Personas (from PMF research)

- **Coach / academy (primary, B2B, India):** batches of match recordings, wants rally review,
  per-player reels for students/parents, per-rally stats. Already records on static cameras.
- **Enthusiast (B2C funnel):** records own matches, wants a highlight reel + basic stats, shares
  with friends. Tripod discipline is the known adoption risk — the UI must teach recording
  guidelines *before* upload, not fail videos after.

## 3. ALPHA — "quick & dirty" (goal: 10–25 invited testers, end-to-end, zero hand-holding)

**Stack:** React + Vite + Tailwind SPA, deployed per HOSTING_PLAN (static hosting, $0).
API = existing FastAPI on the GPU box via tunnel at `api.khelsutra.guru`.
**Auth:** Cloudflare Access email-allowlist (OTP), free ≤50 users, **zero auth code** — backend
reads the authenticated-email header for per-user scoping (alpha-grade tenancy: header is
trustworthy because the tunnel only accepts Access-authenticated traffic).

### Alpha screens (6)

| # | Screen | What it does | Backed by |
|---|--------|--------------|-----------|
| A1 | **Matches** (home) | User's videos, status chip per video (verdict ✅/⚠️/❌), upload CTA | `videos` + owner scoping (B4) |
| A2 | **Upload & Process** | Drag-drop; inline recording-guidelines checklist (pre-upload education from VIDEO_RECORDING_GUIDELINES); chunked upload; then auto-starts processing | B2 upload endpoint → B1 job |
| A3 | **Job progress** | Stage list (validation → segmentation → AI confirm → report) with live status; verdict banner on completion | B1 `GET /api/jobs/{id}` poll |
| A4 | **Rally review** ⭐ | Video player + rally list; click rally → seek & play that span; per-rally **Accept / Reject / nudge start−end ±0.5 s**; bulk "all good"; chips: duration, shot count (+confidence) | B6 correction endpoints → `candidate_segments.human_label/notes` — **the corpus engine** |
| A5 | **Highlights** | Select accepted rallies → name reel → compile → download; list of past reels | `/api/compile` + B3 download endpoint |
| A6 | **Report** | Renders the customer-audience report (verdict, notes, FAQ refs); "nerd view" toggle shows technical.md | existing reporting artifacts |

### Backend work the alpha actually requires (honest dependency list)

| ID | Item | Notes |
|----|------|-------|
| B1 | **Async job model — DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16** | The committed next PLATFORM slice; `POST /api/process` → 202 + `job_id`, `jobs` table (user_version 1→2), ThreadPoolExecutor, `GET /api/jobs/{id}`. **Approving this proposal = greenlighting #16.** A 5–60 min blocking HTTP call cannot sit behind a tunnel UI. |
| B2 | **Chunked upload endpoint** | `POST /api/upload` accepting ≤90 MB parts (free-tier proxy body limit is 100 MB), assemble → MD5 → register under `allowed_video_root/<user>/`. Size cap (e.g. 4 GB) + mp4/mov allowlist. |
